# Arithmetic hypergeometric $\mathcal {D}$-modules and exponential sums on reductive groups

**arXiv ID**: 2608.00470v1
**Authors**: Lei Fu, Xuanyou Li, Chenhan Liu
**Published**: 2026-08-01
**Categories**: math.AG, math.NT
**HTML URL**: https://arxiv.org/html/2608.00470v1

## Abstract

For a finite family of representations of a reductive group, we define a Laurent polynomial on the group. The exponential sum associated this Laurent polynomial is called a hypergeometric exponential sum. We introduce an arithmetic hypergeometric $\mathcal D$-module to study the hypergeometric exponential sum. It is an overholonomic arithmetic $\mathcal D$-module with a Frobenius structure so that the trace of the Frobenius at a rational point is the exponential sum. Over the locus where the Laurent polynomial is nondegenerate, the arithmetic hypergeometric $\mathcal {D}$-module defines an $F$-isocrystal overconvergent along the degenerate locus. As an application, we get an estimation of the hypergeometric exponential sum.

## Full Text

Arithmetic hypergeometric 𝒟-modules and exponential sums on reductive groups

## Arithmetic hypergeometric𝒟\mathcal{D}-modules and exponential sums on reductive groupsLei FuYau Mathematical Sciences Center, Tsinghua University, Beijing 100084, P. R. Chinaleifu@tsinghua.edu.cn,Xuanyou LiQiuzhen College, Tsinghua University, Beijing 100084, P. R. Chinalixuanyo21@mails.tsinghua.edu.cnandChenhan LiuTsinghua University, Beijing 100084, P. R. ## Abstract.

For a finite family of representations of a reductive group, we define a Laurent polynomial on the group.
The exponential sum associated this Laurent polynomial is called a hypergeometric exponential sum. We introduce an
arithmetic hypergeometric𝒟\mathcal{D}-module to study the hypergeometric exponential sum. It is an overholonomic arithmetic𝒟\mathcal{D}-module with a Frobenius structure so that the trace of the Frobenius at a rational point
is the exponential sum. Over the locus where the Laurent polynomial is nondegenerate,
the arithmetic hypergeometric𝒟\mathcal{D}-module defines anFF-isocrystal overconvergent along the degenerate locus.
As an application, we get an estimation of the hypergeometric exponential sum.

## Key words and phrases:arithmetic𝒟\mathcal{D}-module, spherical variety, Fourier transformation.

## 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification:Primary 14F10, 14F30; Secondary 14M27, 11L07

## Theorem 0.2.

Notation as above.
Suppose the morphismG→∏j=1NEnd​(Vj),g↦(ρ1​(g),…,ρN​(g))G\to\prod_{j=1}^{N}\mathrm{End}(V_{j}),\quad g\mapsto(\rho_{1}(g),\ldots,\rho_{N}(g))

is quasi-finite, and has a good equivariant compactification in the sense of Assumptions2.1and4.3.
For any finite extensionk′k^{\prime}ofkkwithq′q^{\prime}element, and anyk′k^{\prime}-pointA=(A1,…,AN)A=(A_{1},\ldots,A_{N})in∏j=1NEnd​(Vj,k)\prod_{j=1}^{N}\mathrm{End}(V_{j,k})such that the Laurent polynomialfA​(g)=∑j=1NTr​(Aj​ρj​(g))f_{A}(g)=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathrm{Tr}(A_{j}\rho_{j}(g))is nondegenerate,
we have|∑g∈G​(k′)ψ​(Trk′/k​(∑j=1NTr​(Aj​ρj​(g))))|≤q′⁣d2​d!​∫Δ∞∩ℭ∏α∈R+λ​(Hα)2ρ​(Hα)2​d​λ,\Big|\sum_{g\in G(k^{\prime})}\psi\Big(\mathrm{Tr}_{k^{\prime}/k}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathrm{Tr}(A_{j}\rho_{j}(g))\Big)\Big)\Big|\leq q^{\prime\frac{d}{2}}d!\int_{\Delta_{\infty}\cap\mathfrak{C}}\prod_{\alpha\in R^{+}}\frac{\lambda(H_{\alpha})^{2}}{\rho(H_{\alpha})^{2}}\mathrm{d}\lambda,

whered=dim​Gd=\mathrm{dim}\,G,ℭ\mathfrak{C}is the dominant Weyl chamber inΛℝ\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}},R+R^{+}is the set of positive roots,{Hα:α∈R}\{H_{\alpha}:\alpha\in R\}is the set of co-roots,ρ=12​∑α∈R+α\rho=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\alpha\in R^{+}}\alpha, andd​λ\mathrm{d}\lambdais the
Lebesgue measure onΛℝ\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}normalized byvol​(Λℝ/Λ)=1\mathrm{vol}(\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}/\Lambda)=1.

In Section 1, after briefly recalling the six-functor formalism on the derived categories of overholonomic arithmetic𝒟\mathcal{D}-modules, we define the hypergeometric𝒟\mathcal{D}-moduleHypπ,!\mathrm{Hyp}_{\pi,!}with a Frobenius structure
so that the trace of Frobenius atAAis exactly the hypergeometric exponential sum (0.0.1). In section 2,
we showHypπ,!\mathrm{Hyp}_{\pi,!}is a direct summand of an explicitly described arithmetic𝒟\mathcal{D}-module,
which we call the modified hypergeometric𝒟\mathcal{D}-module. After describing invariant differential operators in Section 3, we
construct in Section 4 a formal model for the modified hypergeometric𝒟\mathcal{D}-module, and prove it is anFF-isocrystal on𝕍kgen\mathbb{V}^{\mathrm{gen}}_{k}overconvergent along the degernate locus. Theorem0.2then follows
from the trace formula and thepp-adic version of Deligne’s theorem on weights proved by Abe-Caro. In the appendix, we prove
several technical results used in the paper.

containing𝕍\mathbb{V},∞=ℙk−𝕍k\infty=\mathbb{P}_{k}-\mathbb{V}_{k}, andℙ^\widehat{\mathbb{P}}the completion ofℙ\mathbb{P}.

be the specialization map,XXan open subset ofℙ​(k⊕𝕍k)\mathbb{P}(k\oplus\mathbb{V}_{k})such that its complementTTis a divisor,F​-isoc†​(X)F\text{-isoc}^{\dagger}(X)the category ofFF-isocrystals onXXoverconvergent alongTTas defined in[B1, Définition 2.3.6], andF-Coh(𝒟ℙ^,ℚ†(†T))F\text{-Coh}(\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{P}},\mathbb{Q}}(^{\dagger}T))the category of coherent𝒟ℙ^,ℚ†(†T)\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{P}},\mathbb{Q}}(^{\dagger}T)-modules with Frobenius structures.

Our main result about the hypergeometic𝒟\mathcal{D}-modules is the following.

## Theorem 1.5.

Suppose the morphismι:G→𝕍,g↦(ρ1​(g),…,ρN​(g))\iota:G\to\mathbb{V},\quad g\mapsto(\rho_{1}(g),\ldots,\rho_{N}(g))

forj≠0j\not=0.

where𝕍^\widehat{\mathbb{V}}is the completion of the affine space𝕍\mathbb{V}.

We will prove the above theorem in forthcoming sections. At the end of this section, we deduce Theorem0.2from Theorem1.5.

We thus have the Fourier transformation𝔉π:Dovholb​(𝕍k)→Dovholb​(𝕍k).\mathfrak{F}_{\pi}:D^{b}_{\mathrm{ovhol}}(\mathbb{V}_{k})\to D^{b}_{\mathrm{ovhol}}(\mathbb{V}_{k}).

## Remark 2.2.

SupposeGGis a split reductive group scheme over a Dedekind domainDDwith fraction fieldKK,

By[BK, 6.2.3], we have a morphismσK:Y~K→Y¯K\sigma_{K}:\tilde{Y}_{K}\to\overline{Y}_{K}satisfying the conditions of2.1overKK. By the standard passing to limit argument, we may assumeσK\sigma_{K}can be extended to anRR-morphismσ:Y~→Y\sigma:\tilde{Y}\to Yafter replacingSpec​D\mathrm{Spec}\,Dby a dense open subset.
By[EGA IV, 12.2.4], the geometrically integral and geometrically normal property of the generic fiber imply
the same property of the fibers over a dense open subset ofSpec​(D)\mathrm{Spec}(D).
Thus2.1hold forR=D𝔪R=D_{\mathfrak{m}}for almost all maximal ideals𝔪\mathfrak{m}.

of the space of right invariant differential operators overKK.

## Theorem 4.23.

𝔉π​(𝒩)|𝕍kgen\mathfrak{F}_{\pi}(\mathcal{N})|_{\mathbb{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{gen}}}is coherent over𝒪𝕍^,ℚ\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{V}},\mathbb{Q}}. ## Proof.

LetU⊂𝕍kgenU\subset\mathbb{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{gen}}be an affine open subset. has a dense image for eachmm. Here the topology onΓ​(U,𝔉π​(𝒩)ℚ(m))\Gamma(U,\mathfrak{F}_{\pi}({\mathcal{N}})^{(m)}_{\mathbb{Q}})is induced by
its𝒟^𝕍^,ℚ(m)\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{V}},\mathbb{Q}}^{(m)}-module structure ([B3, 4.1.1]). By Corollary4.20and[B3, 4.1.2], it is equivalent to the topology induced by its𝒪𝕍^,ℚ\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{V}},\mathbb{Q}}-module structure.
By[BGR, 3.7.3.1], the map (4.23.1) has closed image. Hence it is surjective. Takinglim→m\varinjlim_{m}, we get an epimorphismΓ​(U,𝔉π​(𝒩)ℚ(0))↠Γ​(U,𝔉π​(𝒩)).\Gamma(U,\mathfrak{F}_{\pi}({\mathcal{N}})^{(0)}_{\mathbb{Q}})\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma(U,\mathfrak{F}_{\pi}({\mathcal{N}})).

So𝔉π​(𝒩)|𝕍kgen\mathfrak{F}_{\pi}(\mathcal{N})|_{\mathbb{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{gen}}}is coherent over𝒪𝕍^,ℚ\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{V}},\mathbb{Q}}.

alongTT. By Proposition4.22and the fact that𝔉π​(𝒩)ℚ(0)→𝔉π​(𝒩)\mathfrak{F}_{\pi}({\mathcal{N}})^{(0)}_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathfrak{F}_{\pi}({\mathcal{N}})is surjective on𝕍kgen\mathbb{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{gen}}, the rank of𝔉π​(𝒩)|𝕍kgen\mathfrak{F}_{\pi}({\mathcal{N}})|_{\mathbb{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{gen}}}does not exceedd!​∫Δ∞∩ℭ∏α∈R+(λ,α)2(ρ,α)2​d​λ.d!\int_{\Delta_{\infty}\cap\mathfrak{C}}\prod_{\alpha\in R^{+}}\frac{(\lambda,\alpha)^{2}}{(\rho,\alpha)^{2}}\mathrm{d}\lambda.∎

## Proof of Theorem1.5.

ThatHypπ,!\mathrm{Hyp}_{\pi,!}is an over-holonomic arithmetic𝒟\mathcal{D}-module is proved in Proposition1.7. By Proposition2.8,Hypπ,!\mathrm{Hyp}_{\pi,!}is a direct factor of𝔉π​(𝒩)\mathfrak{F}_{\pi}(\mathcal{N}). The
other assertions aboutHypπ,!\mathrm{Hyp}_{\pi,!}follow from Theorem4.23. The assertions forHypπ,+\mathrm{Hyp}_{\pi,+}follow by duality.

letH=G×GH=G\times Gact onGGvia the left and the right multiplication ofGG. Supposeσ:Y~→Y\sigma:\tilde{Y}\to Yis anRR-morphism

- (1)

BothY~\tilde{Y}andYYareRR-schemes withHH-action, and containGGas an open subscheme.

σ\sigmais equivariant proper dominant and induces identity onGG.
- (3)

Y~→Spec​R\tilde{Y}\to\mathrm{Spec}\,Ris smooth and has geometrically connected fibers.

ThenRi​σ∗​ωY~=0R^{i}\sigma_{*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}}=0

for anyi≥1i\geq 1, and(σ∗​ωY~)⊗Rk​(𝔭)≅σk​(𝔭)⁣∗​ωY~k​(𝔭)(\sigma_{*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}})\otimes_{R}k(\mathfrak{p})\cong\sigma_{k(\mathfrak{p})*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{k(\mathfrak{p})}}

for any prime ideal𝔭\mathfrak{p}ofRR, where(σ∗​ωY~)⊗Rk​(𝔭)(\sigma_{*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}})\otimes_{R}k(\mathfrak{p}),σk​(𝔭)\sigma_{k(\mathfrak{p})}andY~k​(𝔭)\tilde{Y}_{k(\mathfrak{p})}are the base changes byR→k​(𝔭)R\to k(\mathfrak{p})ofσ∗​ωY~\sigma_{*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}},σ\sigmaandY~\tilde{Y}, respectively.

## Proof.

Localizing at each maximal ideal ofRR, we may assumeRRhas only one maximal ideal𝔪\mathfrak{m}.
For the prime ideal𝔭=0\mathfrak{p}=0, the assertion follows directly from LemmaA.4.
It remains to treat the case𝔭=𝔪\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{m}.
LetRn=R/𝔪n+1R_{n}=R/\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}for each integern≥0n\geq 0,
and letσn:Y~n→Yn\sigma_{n}:\tilde{Y}_{n}\to Y_{n}be the base change byR→RnR\to R_{n}ofσ:Y~→Y\sigma:\tilde{Y}\to Y.
We first proveRi​σn⁣∗​ωY~n=0R^{i}\sigma_{n*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n}}=0for alli≥1i\geq 1andσn⁣∗​ωY~n⊗RnRn−1≅σn−1,∗​ωY~n−1\sigma_{n*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n}}\otimes_{R_{n}}R_{n-1}\cong\sigma_{n-1,*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n-1}}

by induction onnn.
Whenn=0n=0, this follows from LemmaA.4. Assumen≥1n\geq 1. Choose a generatorϖ\varpifor𝔪\mathfrak{m}.
We have a long exact sequence0\displaystyle 0→R0​σ0⁣∗​ωY~0→ϖnR0​σn⁣∗​ωY~n→R0​σn−1,∗​ωY~n−1→⋯\displaystyle\to R^{0}\sigma_{0*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{0}}\xrightarrow{\varpi^{n}}R^{0}\sigma_{n*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n}}\to R^{0}\sigma_{n-1,*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n-1}}\to\cdots→Ri​σ0⁣∗​ωY~0→Ri​σn⁣∗​ωY~n→Ri​σn−1,∗​ωY~n−1→⋯\displaystyle\to R^{i}\sigma_{0*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{0}}\to R^{i}\sigma_{n*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n}}\to R^{i}\sigma_{n-1,*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n-1}}\to\cdots

By the induction hypothesis we haveRi​σ0⁣∗​ωY~0=Ri​σn−1,∗​ωY~n−1=0(i≥1).R^{i}\sigma_{0*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{0}}=R^{i}\sigma_{n-1,*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n-1}}=0\quad(i\geq 1).

So we haveRi​σn⁣∗​ωY~n=0R^{i}\sigma_{n*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n}}=0for alli≥1i\geq 1and we have a short exact sequence0→σ0⁣∗​ωY~0→ϖnσn⁣∗​ωY~n→σn−1,∗​ωY~n−1→0.0\to\sigma_{0*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{0}}\xrightarrow{\varpi^{n}}\sigma_{n*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n}}\to\sigma_{n-1,*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n-1}}\to 0.

Taking the composite of the epimorphismsσm⁣∗​ωY~m↠σn−1,∗​ωY~m−1\sigma_{m*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{m}}\twoheadrightarrow\sigma_{n-1,*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{m-1}}(m≤n)(m\leq n), we get an epimorphismσn⁣∗​ωY~n↠σ0⁣∗​ωY~0.\sigma_{n*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n}}\twoheadrightarrow\sigma_{0*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{0}}.

So we may identify the image ofσ0⁣∗​ωY~0→ϖnσn,∗​ωY~n\sigma_{0*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{0}}\xrightarrow{\varpi^{n}}\sigma_{n,*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n}}with the image ofσn,∗​ωY~n→ϖnσn,∗​ωY~n\sigma_{n,*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n}}\xrightarrow{\varpi^{n}}\sigma_{n,*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n}}.
Henceσn⁣∗​ωY~n⊗RnRn−1≅σn−1,∗​ωY~n−1\sigma_{n*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n}}\otimes_{R_{n}}R_{n-1}\cong\sigma_{n-1,*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}_{n-1}}.

## Corollary A.6.

Assume2.1holds. ThenRi​ι¯∗​ωY~=0R^{i}\bar{\iota}_{*}\omega_{\tilde{Y}}=0for alli≥1i\geq 1.

Note thatι¯\bar{\iota}is a composite ofσ:Y~→Y¯\sigma:\tilde{Y}\to\overline{Y}and

## Corollary A.7.

Assume4.3holds. Denote the compositeY′→X′→𝕍′Y^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}\to\mathbb{V}^{\prime}byf′f^{\prime}. Thenfk⁣∗′​ωYk′f^{\prime}_{k*}\omega_{Y^{\prime}_{k}}is Cohen-Macaulay, and(f′​ωY~′)⊗Rk≅fk⁣∗′​ωYk′(f^{\prime}\omega_{\tilde{Y}^{\prime}})\otimes_{R}k\cong f^{\prime}_{k*}\omega_{Y^{\prime}_{k}}.

Note thatf′f^{\prime}is a finite morphism. The Cohen-Macaulay property follows from LemmaA.4and[S2, Proposition IV.11]. The second assertion follows from PropositionA.5applied
